**What the studies showed (TASK ANATOMY, WHAT THE TEXTS CONTAIN, SUCCESS GIVEN EACH PIECE OF INFORMATION, FAILURE STUDY, DIRECTION 1 STUDY, OPTION 2 STUDY):** public tasks are PR descriptions; 20% need exact text only the hidden test knows (3% solved); fix size rules (1 file <= 20 lines no-exact-text 57%); texts rarely hold grading criteria (all asserted values in 5%); on small winnable fixes an example input 90% vs 54% and the behaviour in words 83% vs 49%; on winnable tasks 65% of failures never change the source (they have a late theory and keep re-checking); runs whose own check SHOWED the problem solve 71% vs 41%; never-edit runs burn one maxed thinking reply (~4.1k tokens, ~130 s, hand-simulation loops).

**LB so far:** pub024 0.18, sk_a2 0.18, sk_a2_32 0.17, mix 0.17, le6_t6 0.15, sq1c2a6 0.12 / 0.10. Timed out: le5, sq1c2, sq1c2t7, srep7, nn32 (every 7-8 min skill-shell agent). Top of LB 0.29, many at 0.24 (an 0.18 agent reaches 0.24 by luck 15% of the time).
**Main findings (11-06/07):** task explains 64% of solved, agent family 0.7%, chance 35%. Wrong edits are in the right file (about 80%) and fail on assertions (83%): logic, not location. Fix size predicts solving (Spearman -0.58), file size barely (-0.19). Only enforced events move behaviour (refusal -> edit 23-39%, warning 6-11%); clock numbers showed no measurable benefit. Hotter sampling hesitates and does not explore (r53). Checklist +10 / +4 (same runs, easy set). No-numbers arms (nn) on 42 tasks: 9-11 solved vs pub024 7, no patch 4-7 vs 21, not significant; their extra edits are mostly wrong. The model skips setup calls: always use a setup agent (le6 pattern), never an "install first" prompt line. "Capped runs never solve" was a label artefact. Garbled skill-shell calls loop and seem not to count against the call limit.
**Paper:** draft not started; user said first reach a better LB agent. Needs the user's go and an answer on the one-account rule (runs used ziad23samer, ziadsamer, fatmazenhom, engziadsamer). Information test pass 2 was due Nov 7 (cutoff Nov 9): status unknown, ask. Evidence and predictions: docs/PAPER_NOTES.md, docs/ARCH_ANALYSIS.md.

- **CONTROL ARM = sk_a2 from 2026-11-21 (user: 'from now on the sk_a2 is the arm we compare to, not b[sb1hl]').** sk_a2 = our best own LB agent (0.18), bundle submissions/sk_a2 unchanged; every new arm runs next to it in the same notebook (memory same-run-control-always).
- **LATENCY CHECK 2026-11-23 (user: 'are we printing something that may be increasing latency; multiple agents time out because of the model loading time, maybe?').** (1) Model loading is outside the task clock: the agent timer starts at context.start_agent_session() right before the agent loop (swegemma agent_runner.py 495), after sandbox setup; vLLM loads once per notebook / scorer run (counts only toward the LB's 12 h total, the same for every agent). The first replies after start are not slower (35 ms per output token in a notebook's first 2 min and later, r138-r142). (2) Printing / prompt size costs ~nothing per reply: least squares over 3,149 requests (r138-r142) seconds = 0.97 + 32.6 ms x output tokens - 0.03 ms x prompt tokens (prefix cache), mean reply 356 output tokens = 11.6 s of 12.3 s: the time is what the model writes (mostly thinking). (3) The one real side cost is compaction: 1.57 summary calls per run x 27 s = 42 s per run (12% of agent time); each summary writes ~726 tokens, about half of it thinking, because the summarizer uses our model settings (thinking_token_budget 2048; 4096 in sk_a2t's runs): it cannot be turned off without turning off the agent's thinking. Our tool outputs are ~31.7k chars per run, 15% of them the repeated MEMORY header + banners (printed twice); trimming them would save maybe 0.2 compactions = ~5 s per run: too small for an arm of its own.

The harness task message states the real budget ('Time allowance: 9 minutes', 32 calls) as for sk_a2_32; the prompt says 8 / 28. **Bars:** >= 1 solve that lands after 480 s or after call 28; mean agent time <= 345 s; before the caps it behaves like the control (first edit call, no source change, empty submits within +1). Pass -> next LB submission (also the real 12 h fit check).

Option T (time + calls, hidden cushion like sk_a2_32): sk_a2_60 with max_time_minutes 9-10 and max_tool_calls 32, prompt and a.py banners unchanged (the model plans for 8 min / 28 calls; only runs that hit a cap get more): estimated +60 s x ~23% of runs at ~8-10% per minute = +1.5-2.5 points (~1 task per 58); mean +14-28 s (332-346 s, at most mix's level); measurable directly in a small run as solves finishing after 480 s or after call 28 (behaviour before the caps is unchanged). Other levers on record: race / best-of-2 +4 points simulated but ADK ParallelAgent compaction crash (closed); information (6/76 -> 30/76 with the real reporter text) has no LB source (hints empty, history tried); prompt / sampling / thinking / tool variants all within luck (r138-r142).
- **RESULT r142 (data/results/2026-11-23_skill_r142_s1 = fatmazenhom/generation-shard-4-gpu, _s2 = ziad23samer/fork-of-fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r142.py, log data/analysis/r142.log). sk_a2_60 5/8, sk_a2v32 2/8: the repro line FAILS; sk_a2v closed as built.** sk_a2v32: time ends 4 vs 1, no source change 5 vs 2, mean 353 vs 355 s; checks in 7/8 runs (19 checks, 28 repro writes). The fail-first event became a loop: YOUR REPRO DOES NOT SHOW THE BUG fired 10 times; the model obeyed 'make it fail' and rewrote the repro again and again instead of taking the 'or make your edit now' half (fastapi_14419: 5 x NOT SHOWN, 5 repro writes, no edit, time end; rich_4079: 12 repro writes, edits refused at the 32-call budget; fastapi_15589: 2 x NOT SHOWN then 3 more repro scripts, time end). When the repro did show the bug (SHOWS 3), it worked: rich_3882 solved in 66 s, fastapi_11194 in 164 s; rich_3470 passed its own repro and was still wrong (self-confirming). Pooled sk_a2v + sk_a2v32: 6/16 vs same-run control 9/16. Lesson: a gate the model must satisfy before editing turns into a loop on tasks whose bug is hard to reproduce (cache / state / rendering bugs); an event with two options is read as the first option. sk_a2_60 (the 60 s command timeout, no behaviour change): 5/8, 1 time end; no hung command this run; it is the base from now on (free fix). LB: sk_a2t still PENDING since 11-22 15:12.
- **WHY WRONG PLACE / WRONG LOGIC (2026-11-23; user: 'v doesn't edit wrongly but not at all ... need another way to address why it gets the location wrong at first place or makes the wrong edit'; scripts/mine_bank/wrong_place_why.py, wrong_logic_shape.py, logs data/analysis/wrong_place_why.log / wrong_logic_shape.log).** (1) Finding is not the problem: lines seen before the first source edit (a.py show / read_file / sed -n output vs the real fix, +-3 lines): solved 98-100% saw them; RIGHT SPOT WRONG LOGIC 98-100%; WRONG SPOT 89-90% (it had the right lines in front of it and changed others); WRONG FILE 43% saw them, ~half never opened the file (sk_a2 family: 7 of 13 on two tasks: rich_4079 -> pretty.py / table.py, fastapi_13920 -> _compat). sk_a2 family wrong edits: 76 right spot wrong logic (64%), 29 wrong spot (25%), 13 wrong file (11%). (2) Not incompleteness: pooled, wrong-logic patches cover fewer real-fix hunks (51% all vs 86%), but within the same task coverage is the same (fastapi_14419 0% / 0%, rich_3676 0 / 0, rich_3521 100 / 100, rich_3470 100 / 100): the pooled gap is task mix; on 1-hunk tasks the wrong patches sit on the same lines and are often bigger (fastapi_15589 3.35x vs 1.01x, rich_3470 2.59 vs 1.83): wrong behaviour, not missed places. (3) Wrong places are late guesses: sk_a2 family by banner state at the first edit: before any banner 81 runs solved 73%, wrong spot 0%, wrong file 1%; after NO EDIT YET 51 runs 55% / 8% / 4%; after STOP READING 130 runs 22% / 18% / 7% (+ 26% whose edit never landed). Partly task difficulty, but every wrong-spot edit came after a banner. Reading: the model locates the code; what it lacks is the expected behaviour (what the hidden test checks). When it does not get it, it keeps reading until the banner and then guesses a nearby place. A wrong guess costs nothing over no edit (both score 0), so the target is understanding the behaviour before the edit, which is what sk_a2v's repro targets (r141: STILL BUG never fired; r142 tests it with the call budget fixed).

Sim 8/8 ok, budgets per arm (60 / 28 and 60 / 32), sk_a2v events through the harness. **Bars:** sk_a2v32: no edit refused by the call budget; no source change and empty submits not above sk_a2_60's + 1; wrong edits per run not above sk_a2_60's; time ends not above + 1; mean <= 400 s; solves >= control + 2 for the LB. sk_a2_60 vs the sk_a2 history: hung commands cost <= 60 s.

Why the no-source runs: rich_3470 and fastapi_15589 sent their edit as call 29-30 -> 'BudgetExceeded: Tool call budget exhausted (28 calls)' with 2-3 minutes still left (348 s / 390 s): the 2-4 repro calls came out of the 28; fastapi_15589 also read 9 more times after REPRO SHOWS THE BUG instead of editing. rich_3180 / rich_3521: the usual hand-trace time ends, never reached the repro (rich_3521 put a diff inside submit_patch's arguments at the end). Next candidate: sk_a2v with max_tool_calls 32 (+ the 60 s command timeout); sk_a2_32 shows 32 calls fit the time (LB 0.17, mean 334 s).
- **HUNG COMMANDS: THE FIX IS OURS (2026-11-22; user: 'how can we fix the hung commands in sk_a2'; scripts/mine_bank/cmd_durations.py, log data/analysis/cmd_durations.log).** The 240 s is our own eval_config.yaml (evaluation.timeout_seconds: 240, in all 234 of our bundles); harness default 300 (swegemma budget.py), applied by run_command as min(timeout, remaining time) with the whole process group killed (README 381, 256), also to run_skill_script (agent_runner.py 323) and announced in the task message ('Single command timeout: N seconds'). Grading (Stage 2, scripts/metric.py, verify_task) is a separate stage; the README scopes eval_config to Stage 1 inference, and the public 'LB 0.22 four config files' bundle (and black-cat, 0-10, shape-of-doubt) ship timeout_seconds: 60 and scored, so a low value does not break grading (verification.py does read config.harness.command_timeout_seconds when run in-process; our own notebooks grade in-process -> local grading would use it too: check before a local run). Durations over the bank (138k tool calls): python inline > 60 s 40 (33 timed out), python script 19 (all 19 timed out), pytest 8 (2 timed out, ~6 finished between 120 and 240 s), a.py 1, everything else ~0; nothing finished between 60 and 90 s. So calls over 60 s are almost only hangs. Timed-out commands: pub024 21, sk_a2 4 / 96 runs, combo1 3. Fix: timeout_seconds 60 in eval_config (no prompt change; the harness tells the model the limit): a hang costs 60 s instead of 240 s; at risk ~6 long pytest runs in 1,597. Not applied yet.
- **WHERE r140's TWO EXTRA SOLVES CAME FROM (2026-11-22; user: 'where did the extra solves come from if not the update'; read call by call from data/results/2026-11-22_skill_r140_s1).** (1) rich_3882 (sk_a2 solved it in all 9 earlier runs): sk_a2's first check script (Prompt.ask, ran WITHOUT the prompt's 'timeout 30') waited for input and was killed by the harness after 240 s at call 8, half the run gone; it then wrote 4 more scripts, never edited, time end. sk_a2r's script used a subclass and returned at once; edit at 53 s (7 garbled edit_file calls, then a python heredoc), solved in 120 s. Not the RUN IT change: a hung command. In the bank a 240 s hung command hit 4/96 sk_a2 runs (2 solved), 1/92 sk_a2_32. sk_a2v's a.py check enforces 30 s on the repro, so it removes this case. (2) fastapi_14419 (a coin-flip task: sk_a2 2 of 3 earlier): both runs read the same code and wrote the same idea (key the dependency cache on scopes only when a dependant uses scopes). sk_a2's uses_scopes also returns True when security_scopes is set (inherited scopes count -> the bug stays); sk_a2r's only counts a SecurityScopes parameter in the signature or a sub-dependency (close to the real fix's _uses_scopes on own scopes). sk_a2r spent 2 long replies (2,084 / 2,139 tokens) on it and never ran a probe. Run-to-run variation, not RUN IT.
- **RUN BUILT r141 (user 2026-11-22: 'yes fail-first in, build it now'): sk_a2 (CONTROL) vs sk_a2r (replicate r140's 5/8) vs sk_a2v on the 8 solvable dev16 in r134's two shards (kaggle/cells_skill_r141_s1 / _s2, only cell2 differs from r140; 12 runs per notebook), CONCURRENCY 2, guard 1.2 h.** sk_a2v = sk_a2 + repro check with verdict events, fail-first (scripts/make_ska2v.py; dist/sk_a2v.zip md5 61d0aac7, pack.py OK): a.py check runs .git/agent/repro.py (timeout 30) and reads a line BUG / FIXED: no change + BUG -> REPRO SHOWS THE BUG (next call the edit); no change + FIXED -> YOUR REPRO DOES NOT SHOW THE BUG (it proves nothing: fix it once or edit now); change + BUG -> STILL BUG (change the edit); change + FIXED -> REPRO PASSES (one test file under timeout 90, then submit; + a note when it never printed BUG before the edit); neither -> NO VERDICT. git status flags kept; the old auto-pytest dropped. Prompt: scoring line says repro (two calls at most) then the edit; Step 3 rewritten as the repro step (issue's exact example, BUG / FIXED on their own lines, run only through a.py check); worked example 1 now has the repro and REPRO SHOWS THE BUG / REPRO PASSES; example 2 shows DOES NOT SHOW THE BUG and STILL BUG. All six outputs tested on real rich code (a 60 s repro is cut at 30 s -> NO VERDICT); sim 12/12 ok, both fail-first events reached the model through the harness (data/sim/armsim_r141_s*.log; armsim matches sk_a2v by prompt text), sk_a2 / sk_a2r files byte-identical to r140. **Bars (set now):** sk_a2v: repro checked through a.py in >= 6/8 runs; DOES NOT SHOW THE BUG followed by a changed repro or an edit; wrong edits per run (fail_place2 method) below sk_a2's; time ends and no patch not above sk_a2's + 1; mean agent <= 400 s; solves >= sk_a2 + 2 for the LB. sk_a2r: pooled with r140 (16 runs) vs sk_a2 (r138-r141, 32 runs); it stays the LB candidate only if it again beats the same-run sk_a2.
- **LB SUBSETS 2026-11-22 (user: 'what subset have good correlation with results on the LB'; scripts/mine_bank/lb_subsets.py, log data/analysis/lb_subsets.log): none, and none can.** Our 10 LB-scored agents (sk_a2 11, pub024 11, sk_a2_32 10, sb1hl / combo1 9, evidc 9 / 7, sa2 / nn32rcn2 7, sq1c2a6 7 / 6, evidefs 6 of 58) spread with variance 3.0 tasks^2, while one score's binomial noise alone is 7.2 (SD 2.7 tasks): 0% of the spread is measurable skill. Rank correlation of the task-matched local gain vs pub024 with the LB: all tasks -0.43, dev16 -0.46, solvable 8 -0.44, mid tasks -0.35, hard +0.27 (7 agents), easy +0.67 (5 agents): sign flips and tiny samples, i.e. noise. The LB cannot rank our agents; decide on large same-run local samples, use the LB only as a sanity check (no LB time-outs, no crash).
- **RESULT r140 (data/results/2026-11-22_skill_r140_s1 = fatmazenhom/generation-shard-4-gpu, _s2 = ziad23samer/fork-of-fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r140.py, log data/analysis/r140.log). sk_a2r 5/8 vs sk_a2 3/8 (+2 meets the solve bar) but the MECHANISM BARS FAIL.** Capped replies 13 (924 s) vs 8 (636 s) (bar <= 2/3); probe runs in 3/8 runs (bar 4/8; 12 probes, 11 of them on rich_3180 / rich_3521); 3 capped replies came right after a probe (it runs the code, then still traces); time ends 2 vs 3 (ok); no source change 3 / 3, empty submits 1 / 1, mean 374 vs 367 s (ok). The two extra solves are not where probes ran: rich_3882 (sk_a2 timed out on a task solved 9/9 before) and fastapi_14419 (no probe, 2 capped replies). P(>= 5 of 8) at sk_a2's pooled 9/24 on these tasks = 0.14 -> likely luck. Replicated in r141.
- **PLAN (not built) 2026-11-22: sk_a2 + repro check (user: 'yes plan it, sk_a2 with the repro check'; evidence scripts/mine_bank/repro_gate.py, log data/analysis/repro_gate.log).** Evidence first: (1) in the sq1c2a8 family (280 runs) the repro almost never CORRECTED an edit: first check after the edit printed FIXED in 149/171 checked runs (solved 93%), BUG -> edited again 4 runs; its low wrong-edit rate comes with early edits (first edit call 10) on the picked e24 tasks, so the gate worked as a correctness SIGNAL, not a fixer. (2) sk_a2 (88 runs): 28 of 78 edited runs never checked after the edit and solved 14% (vs 52% with a check), but those are late edits (median call 22.5, 4 calls left, 6 time ends): timing, not the missing check. sk_a2 already spends ~2.5 script runs + 1.8 scratch writes BEFORE its first edit (runs with a check before the edit 44% solved vs 30% without), and its check outputs rarely give a clear verdict (28 'other', 1 FIXED). Design (sk_a2v, one change set, everything else sk_a2): the repro uses calls sk_a2 already spends; a.py gets the 'check' command (exists in a.py; trimmed to the repro only, timeout 30, no auto pytest) with verdict events: before any source change, repro prints BUG -> 'REPRO SHOWS THE BUG: now make the edit'; prints FIXED or nothing -> 'YOUR REPRO DOES NOT SHOW THE BUG: it proves nothing; make it fail the way the issue says, or edit now'; after the edit, BUG -> 'STILL BUG: your edit did not fix this case: change the edit' ; FIXED -> 'REPRO PASSES: run one test file under timeout 90, then submit_patch'. Prompt: repro.py rule (the issue's own example, exact names / messages, prints FIXED or BUG), the four events with one worked example each (few-shot for every event), check comes from a.py only. Risks: time (sk_a2 time ends 13-19%), self-confirming repros (the fail-first event targets them), hidden-text tasks unaffected. Bars to set before the run: repro written in >= 6/8 runs; fail-first event seen and followed; wrong edits per run (fail_place2 method) below sk_a2's; time ends / no patch not above sk_a2 + 1; solves >= sk_a2 + 2 for the LB. Open for the user: fail-first part in or out; run next to r140's sk_a2r or after.
- **WRONG-EDIT RANKING 2026-11-22 (user: 'i need to know what agent that had least of those failure patterns'; scripts/mine_bank/fail_place.py (.diff method, runs with a saved patch) + fail_place2.py (all runs: the edit place read from the agent's own edit calls; log data/analysis/fail_place2.log)).** Reachable tasks only (31 tasks solved by >= 2 agents, drops most hidden-exact-text / big-feature tasks). Each failed run bucketed vs the real fix: no source change, WRONG FILE, WRONG SPOT (right file, not near the real-fix lines), RIGHT SPOT WRONG LOGIC. Check of the text method against the .diff method (268 runs with both): same bucket 169, right spot read as wrong spot 34, edit not recognised 59 -> rough, leans to 'wrong spot'. **Task-matched vs pub024 (wrong edits per run, agent vs pub024):** fewest with real samples = the sq family: sq2 2% vs 12% (36 runs), sq1c2a8 4% vs 12% (48 runs; solved 79% vs 68%; P(<= 2 of 48 at 12%) = 0.06), sq1c2a6c / f6rc / a6rnt 8%, sq1c2a8rc 9% (80 runs), sa2 12% vs 12%; agents with 0-5% but many more no-edit runs (sk_nav_s6_32 0% / no source 50%, bo3 4% / 42%) avoid wrong edits by not editing. **sk_a2 is among the most: 21% vs 11% (wrong place 12% vs 4%, logic 9% vs 8%; P(>= 12 of 59 at 11%) = 0.03)**, sk_a2_32 16%, sk_fr2 22%, sb1hl 24% vs 24% (mostly logic), combo1 27%: the agents that edit most get more wrong edits; sk_a2 turns its no-patch runs into wrong-place edits. The low-wrong-edit sq1c2 family = fixer + submitter (SequentialAgent), clock line in every shell output, edit by 180 s, a repro .git/agent/repro_a.py that must print FIXED, then REPRO PASSES ends the work (one test file, finish). Caveats: the sq family ran on e24 (tasks picked as solvable; their prompts were tuned there), sq1c2a6 (6-min sibling) scored 0.12 / 0.10 on the LB (clock family: LB timeouts, LESSONS 4), and the text method is rough. Lesson for sk_a2: what separates the low-wrong-edit agents is a run that checks the behaviour (repro prints FIXED) before finishing, not where they read.
- **sk_a2 DEEP DIVE 2026-11-22 (user: 'why is it our top LB and what does it do, deep investigation'; scripts/mine_bank/ska2_deep.py (one row per logged 31B run: 4,482 runs, 181 agents -> data/analysis/ska2_deep_runs.jsonl) + ska2_lb.py; logs data/analysis/ska2_deep.log / ska2_lb.log; banners: ska2_banner.py).** What it is: mix (bf prompt + r4 decision rule / example / edit fallback, built 10-25) + a reading tool installed by one skill call (.git/agent/a.py: find / outline / show, MEMORY header of lines read, ALREADY SHOWN on repeats, NO EDIT YET at 10 looks / 210 s and STOP READING at 15 / 300 s while git status is clean) + thinking 2048 / max output 4096, T 0.2, 28 calls, 8 min. Anatomy per run (vs pub024): install 1.0 (0), find 4.6 (search 7.4), outline 1.2, show 9.2 (read_file 9.1), raw cat/sed 0.1 (0.8), source edits 2.5 (2.3), own scripts 1.8 + runs 2.6 (3.8), submit 0.9. **Task-matched vs pub024 (48 tasks, 88 vs 452 runs): solve 36% vs 35% (tasks better 8, worse 8); no patch 15% vs 28%; time ends 13% vs 21%; edited 92% vs 86%; agent 285 vs 339 s; 10.0 vs 11.7 s per reply; replies >= 3,500 tokens 0.1% vs 1.5%; but capped replies (>= 88% of its 2,048 thinking) 0.72 vs 0.30 per run.** vs sb1hl (12 tasks) solve 35/36%, no patch 18/36%; vs sa2 (28) 61/68%; vs sk_a2_32 (40) 43/42%. Every sk_a2 variant in the bank (34 arms: sk_a, sk_ad/ad2, a4, a5/a5r, t05, b/b2k, nav*, p1, fr/fr2, r138's six, nn3x) is task-matched within luck of it; none clearly above. Banners: NO EDIT YET seen in 60/88 runs, an edit within 2 calls after it 9, an edit at any later point 52 (they do not cause the edit at once; edits come ~call 16 median anyway). Outcome by first edit: never 12% (0 solved), <= call 12 28% (52%), 13-20 35% (42%), > 20 24% (19%). **Why it tops the LB: not shown to be skill.** LB scores are floor(solved / 58): sk_a2 11, pub024 11, mix 10, sk_a2_32 10, sb1hl / combo1 / evidc / le6_t6 9, sa2 / nn32rcn2 / sq1c2a6 / evidc 7, evidefs 6. If all ten of these were the same agent (pooled 8.5 / 58), one scoring >= 11 has p 0.22 and the best of 12 submissions reaching 11 has p 0.95; sa2 is +3% vs pub024 locally and scored 7. Local solves do not predict our LB order. What is real about sk_a2 (large samples, task-matched): it reaches an edit more often and sooner, leaves fewer empty patches, finishes ~55 s faster, never writes very long replies. Those turn into the same solves because the extra edits are wrong (10-26 WRONG-EDIT ANALYSIS: of 30 edited-but-failed runs, A hidden exact text 10, B big feature 9, C wrong place 7, D wrong logic 4). Building on it: keep its frame (fast, edits, few empty patches); gains have to come from edit correctness (C + D) or the hand-trace time-outs (r140), not from more pressure to edit.

- **WHY THE EXPECTATION COMMAND FAILS + A's harness story (2026-11-20, user: 'what happens regarding the harness' / 'what is the solution to the expectation command'; scripts/mine_bank/expect_textcall.py, write_file_rel.py):** (1) The harness (swegemma agent_runner.py ~L640-740): a turn that ends without a tool call and without submit_patch gets a nudge message and the ROOT agent is run again; 'tool call finished closing' when the reply holds '<|tool_call>', 'Please continue ...' otherwise; at most 3 nudges in a row, but the counter resets on any turn with a tool call. In sbE4A every re-run starts the SequentialAgent again, so the search stage ran again each time (its call resets the counter: no 3-nudge stop), and ADK's end-of-run compaction on the SequentialAgent root crashed once the session passed the threshold -> 'sandbox'. sbE4A per run: rich_3278 x2 solved (the solver made short calls first, expectation at 49-50 s, edit, submit); fastapi_15589 x2 and fastapi_12942 p2: the first solver reply was the expectation as text 4-5 times -> crash; fastapi_11194 p1: the first reply was the expectation as a ```python block until the 8,192-token cap -> nudge -> again -> crash; fastapi_11194 p2: same first reply, then a real expectation after the nudge, 'passes before any edit', never edited, time; fastapi_12942 p1: expectation at 8 s (real), 'shown' x5, never edited, time. (2) Over r127-r129, 33 of 66 expectation attempts came out as text; as the solver's first call 19/21 (90%), after a real call 14/45 (31%); braces / f-strings barely matter; every body > 400 chars has double quotes. The fixed edit command (~900 chars) is not affected: the expectation is the model's own long code inside a shell heredoc. (3) The harness's write_file over the whole bank: 4,624 calls, sent as text 6 (0.1%), 'mandatory input parameters' 461 (10%, an error result the model can retry), other errors 282, content median 792 chars (90th 1,660). **Proposed fix (not built):** write expect.py with write_file (tool argument, not a heredoc), run it with a short fixed command that prints the before-edit events, and move the after-edit events into the edit step (fixed text the model copies reliably); keep a short call before it.
- **RESULT r129 (data/results/2026-11-20_skill_r129_p1 = ziad23samer/fork-of-fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu, _p2 = ziad23samer/getting-started-gemma-4-developer-agent-new; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r129.py, log data/analysis/r129.log). sbE3b 3/8, sbE4A 2/8, sbE4B 3/8 (~4.6 expected at bank rates). The main loss is the EXPECTATION COMMAND SENT AS TEXT: 6 of 24 runs sunk.** Every text reply in r129 is the expectation command, and nearly all end 'bash .git/agent/check.sh' + the examples' ``` fence (+ <tool_call|>): the call cannot be parsed, the harness says '... tool call finished closing', the model resends the identical text (T 0.2). sbE4A: the solver's FIRST call is the expectation, as designed, and came out as text in 4/8 runs (14 replies, all before any real call); each text reply ends the turn, the harness re-runs the root (the search stage ran again each time), and ADK's end-of-run compaction then crashes on the SequentialAgent root ('No LlmAgent model available for event compaction summarizer', google/adk/apps/compaction.py _ensure_compaction_summarizer gets app.root_agent) -> 4 runs 'sandbox', no patch (bank: this crash 1 other run ever, evroute; evidc 0/229). r128's one text-call run was also the expectation as the first call. sbE3b p2 rich_3278 and sbE4B p1 rich_3278 were also sunk by it. sbE4B's resend rule: 4 closing messages, the next reply text again 4/4 (FAIL); no edit undone (0). Backslash fallback: its note fired 5 times, each edit applied (PASS where reached). sbE4A stage: replied with lines 20/22 (incl. re-runs), but the solver's expectation-first then failed as text. Never edited: sbE3b 4/8, sbE4A 6/8, sbE4B 4/8 (bar 2/8 FAIL). **Expectation line vs base on these 4 tasks (bank):** expectation arms r127-r129 15/44 (34%), sb1h / sb1hl 9/26 (35%), sb1 (edit_file, older base) 19/28 (68%; fastapi_15589 13/16 vs the heredoc family 1/2 and the expectation arms 3/14) - small samples, but the heredoc base itself may be weaker on these tasks. **Proposed next (not built):** remove the ``` fences from every example command (the fence is what gets copied; 23 of 29 bank text replies end with it) and end each example command with a harmless line, so a copied marker becomes a shell comment instead of an unparseable call; and a same-run sb1 vs sb1hl check on these tasks.

Builder scripts/make_expect_e4.py, prompts scripts/agent_expect/make_prompts_e4.py (written fresh from sbE3's Reader story). **sbE3b** = sbE3 + the backslash fallback in the edit step (scripts/edit_fallback.py) + a worked example of its note (13,952 chars). **sbE4A** = sbE3b + a search stage first: SequentialAgent [search (thinking off, one run_skill_script call of a cut-down evidence script: where each name is defined, or used in source when it has no def / class line, + up to 8 test lines that use it; no history, no changelog; a second call refused; reply <= 12 copied lines, output_key evidence; prompt 2,372 chars with a worked example) -> solver (sbE3b whose prompt carries the lines under WHERE THE CODE AND ITS TESTS ARE, step 2 'the search is already done', example 1 starts with the expectation; 14,147 chars)], 29 calls (28 + the stage's one) as evidc. **sbE4B** = sbE3b + WHEN lines and worked examples for the harness's '... tool call finished closing' message (resend the same call ending right after its last line; bank: calls as text in 10 of 334 runs, 7 of them ended with no source change, 23 of 29 end with the ``` fence; scripts/mine_bank/b_losses.py) and for an existing test failing after the edit (fix forward, never undo with git checkout / stash / reset; bank: 4 runs wiped an edit, 1 lost its fix) (15,462 chars). The budget-warning nudge for never-edit runs was not repeated (r87 evide_warn 2/8). Checks: scripts/mine_bank/e4_cmd_check.py 24/24 (sbE3's 15 checks with the new edit step + 9 on the search script), edit_fallback_check 10/10. Sim (data/sim/armsim_r129.log; SIM_PRE_CMDS = a scratch regex line, the doubled-backslash edit before any expectation, the expectation, the same edit, the re-run): 6/6 runs end ok; held back once per run; the second send applied through the fallback with the intended line; the stage's second call refused; all solver requests carry the full prompts (+ ADK's name line), sbE4A's solver has the stage lines pinned (31/31). **Bars (set now; 8 runs per arm; luck band ~ +-2 solves per arm, so solves alone decide nothing):** fallback: no rich_3278 run loses its edit to doubled-backslash 'found 0' (r128: p2 lost); A: the stage replies with lines (not NOTHING RELEVANT) in >= 6/8 and its median time <= 30 s; the solver's first call is the expectation in >= 4/8; first expectation median >= 60 s earlier than sbE3b's; first applied edit median earlier than sbE3b's; B: after a 'finished closing' message the next reply is a real call in >= 2/3 of those cases; no applied edit undone with checkout / stash / reset; all arms: the hold-back is followed by the expectation (r128 3/3); never-edit <= 2/8 per arm. Read with scripts/mine_bank/r128.py adapted to r129.
- **BACKSLASH FALLBACK BUILT (user 2026-11-20: 'okay let the edit step undo the doubling'; not in a bundle yet):** scripts/edit_fallback.py halve(step) adds to the heredoc edit step: old text found 0 times as sent + it has a double backslash + found exactly once with each double backslash read as one -> use that old text and the new text halved the same way, print 'double backslashes in your text read as single ones', then the usual 'old text found 1 times' / EDITED. No backslash in the step's own text (chr(92)). Checks scripts/mine_bank/edit_fallback_check.py 10/10 on sb1hl's and sbE3's steps (doubled -> applied with the intended line; exact text unchanged; wrong text stays found 0; halved text twice -> not applied; plain edits unchanged). - **DIRECTION 1 STUDY: THE MODEL'S OWN CHECKS 2026-11-20 (user: 'yes go deeper on direction 1, and think out of many arms and many ideas'; scripts/mine_bank/repro_study.py, log data/analysis/repro_study.log; 1,090 logged runs on 28 winnable-core tasks).** Runs whose reasoning says a check SHOWED the problem: 71% solved (177 runs) vs 41% for runs that ran checks but never did (728); calling the hidden test's entry point does not matter (45% vs 50%); combined, shown + other entry 76%, shown + same entry 67%, not shown 40-43%. Never-edited runs start checking early (first check median 37 s) but show the problem in only 9% (solved runs 23%); when shown (median 125 s) an edit followed in 144/177. **Reading:** the missing piece is an expected value: the model's checks print results and it judges them 'correct'; an assert with the wanted result makes 'shown' mechanical. **Arm ideas (not built; for discussion):** E1 sb1hle 'expectation first': the first concrete step writes .git/agent/expect.py (asserts of the wanted behaviour, shaped like the closest existing test) through a fixed short command that runs it and prints an event: '== EXPECTATION FAILS: the problem is shown, edit now' / '== EXPECTATION PASSES before any edit: it does not show the problem yet; change it to what the issue says must change' (stops re-checking loops); sb1h's edit step re-runs expect.py after every edit ('PASSES now' / 'STILL FAILS'); this is the new-behaviour test r125 lacked, and the first event channel BEFORE the first edit. E2 sb1hlen = E1 + a late nudge on the same runner (baseline-commit clock): no edit yet late in the run -> '== no edit yet: edit the likeliest cause now; the expectation will tell'. E3 title patterns: worked examples turning one-line titles into concrete expectations ('Fix X and Y' -> X and Y together break; 'Add support for Z' -> Z currently raises / is ignored; 'Show a clear error' -> pytest.raises). E4 two candidate expectations for one-line titles; keep the one that fails. E5 expectation copied from the closest existing test (the hidden test extends an existing file in 85/129). E6 = E1 on T 1.0 if r126 passes. Keep the prompt near sb1hl's size (the runner is a few bash lines).

Examples: green edit -> reproduce check; an edit breaking test_total_plain_items -> edited again with getattr(item, 'quantity', 1) -> green; found 0 (text, no second command); fence error; budget. **Harness check:** swegemma masks pytest only when enable_sandbox_testing is False; the official HARNESS_README says the default is True (pytest available in Container A) - so the step should work on the scorer (2 vCPU / 4 GiB containers: tests may be slower; 60 s cap). Checks (scripts/mine_bank/autotest_cmd_check.py with a pytest-enabled python, log data/analysis/autotest_cmd_check.log): 12/12 (green edit not blaming an old failing test, breaking edit named + output, syntax error reported, found 0 unchanged, test-file edit silent, no test file silent, dotted-path fallback, uninstalled src layout silent, __init__ -> folder name, > 60 s test silent with the edit applied, no cache / bytecode files left). Sim 4/4 ok, prompts exact (the sim never sends heredoc edits, so no repository tests ran locally). **Bars (set now; 8 v 8):** delivery: a test line after >= 60% of applied edits; use: after a '== THIS EDIT MAKES EXISTING TESTS FAIL' the next edit addresses the named test in >= 50% of cases (hand-read) and >= 1 run turns it green; harm: agent s <= sb1hl + 30, compactions <= sb1hl + 0.5 per run, runs changing source >= sb1hl - 2, no patch <= sb1hl + 1, time ends <= sb1hl + 2; solves luck. Decision: mechanism works + no harm -> the 12-task run.
- **IDEA A (auto test after an edit) OFFLINE CHECK 2026-11-20 (user: 'yes do the offline check for idea A'; scripts/mine_bank/autotest_check.py, log data/analysis/autotest_check.log; the model's own pytest runs in r118-r124, sb1h family, 104 runs with an applied source edit; no repository code run here).** (1) Would it catch wrong fixes? Of 33 edited-but-not-solved runs, only 10 ran the existing tests after their edit: at their last run 4 still showed failures (catchable, and left red) and 5 were all green (a wrong fix the existing file cannot catch: the hidden test is new); 23 never ran tests after the edit = what the step would add -> expect roughly 40% of those to see a red result. Solved runs: 54/71 ran tests after an edit; the first run showed failures in 9 and 4 of them were turned green later (the model does act on red tests); 5 solved runs still ended red (pre-existing failures / missing fixtures = noise the step must not over-sell). (2) Which file: the name rule (test_<module>.py / <module>_test.py) finds the hidden test's file on 5/9 tasks with a public patch (policyuniverse, pyirf, geopandas, peewee, compress_pickle; misses pySHACL test_manual_targeting, pint-pandas test_issues, lizard x2 testCAndCPP); the model's own choices hit it more often (peewee test_auto, compress_pickle test_utils, lizard testCAndCPP, pint test_issues) -> the rule needs a fallback (test files that import the edited module) or the model's last-run test file. (3) Cost: the tests are fast: 97 pytest outputs, median 0.12 s, p90 1.1 s, max 9.6 s. **Assessment:** cheap and safe to run, but the expected gain is modest: about 23 of 104 edited runs get a new test result, ~40% of those red, and the model must still fix it in the little time wrong-fix runs have left -> a few solves per hundred runs at best; the file choice is the weak point.
- **WHERE sb1h / sb1hl FAIL 2026-11-20 (user: 'but can't we make it better? the sb1h lite'; scripts/mine_bank/fail_modes.py, log data/analysis/fail_modes.log; r123 + r124, 64 runs).** Solved 35; failed 29 = NO SOURCE EDIT 16 (8 ended by time, 4 hit the call budget; their longest single reply median 130 s) + WRONG FIX 13 (changed source, not solved). Wrong-fix runs are late and short of checks: agent s median 426 vs 283 for solved, calls median 28 vs 24, and the existing test file was run after the last edit in only 4/13 vs 26/35 solved runs (the reproduce heredoc re-run 9/13 vs 29/35) -> partly reverse-causal (they run out of time), but it matches the bank (a python check after the last edit 57% vs 32% solved, pytest 50 vs 42%). Per task: peewee 6 wrong fixes of 8 (the known Column.__init__ signature failure), fastapi_11194 5 no-edit of 8, rich_3506 4/8, pySHACL 3/4. Already closed: thinking caps (r111: replies got slower), an LLM check stage (r63-r65). Hidden test file vs the edited module's name (2,536 SWE-rebench tasks with a source fix + a test change): test_<module>.py / <module>_test.py 51%, a test file name containing the module name 60%. Ideas under discussion (not built): (1) the edit step itself runs the matching existing test file right after an applied edit (an event the model cannot skip, no extra call); (2) an event that pushes the first edit earlier, using the baseline-commit clock.

- **RUN BUILT r122: sb1h (control) vs sbG on r120's 4 tasks (fastapi_11194, peewee_migrate-203, rich_3506, rich_3521), ONE notebook (kaggle/cells_skill_r122, only cell2 differs from r121) for both accounts = 2 passes, CONCURRENCY 2, guard 0.8 h.** User 2026-11-20: 'yes verify the timing and build it on sb1h'. **Timing verified (sim, real harness, data/sim/armsim_baseline_age.log):** the `_swegemma_baseline` commit time = the first model request time - 0 or 1 s in 4/4 runs (commit message 'baseline' in this harness version; looked up by the tag). **sbG** (scripts/make_sbG.py; prompt scripts/agent_sbG/system.md from scripts/agent_sbG/make_prompt.py, 3,223 words vs sb1h 1,503) = sb1h whose edit command writes its fixed step to .git/agent/step.py and runs it: replace only when found once (sb1h); while the run is young (now - baseline commit time < 360 s; the number lives only in the step's code, not in the prose), the FIRST edit into a function (smallest def around the old text in HEAD; the old lines when module-level / not Python / unparseable) is NOT applied: '== NOT EDITED YET. How this function changed before, newest first:' + the last 3 commits (title + up to 15 +/- lines each, sbF2's filter, done in Python) + '== If a commit above fixed the same kind of problem, send the edit again changed to do it the same way; if one made the change the issue complains about, send it again so it undoes that change. Otherwise apply it as it is: python3 .git/agent/step.py FILE'. Later edits into a shown function, own new lines, late edits, runs without the tag: applied as sb1h, no history (history after an edit changed 0 edits in r119-r121). Examples: 3 = held with a same-kind commit -> sent again changed (ParseError) -> applied; 4 = found 0 -> re-read -> held with unrelated history -> apply as it is; 5 = found 2 -> def line -> held, the newest commit made the change the issue complains about, the edit already undoes it -> apply as it is; + new file / cut-apart / budget. Checks (scripts/mine_bank/gate_cmd_check.py, log data/analysis/gate_cmd_check.log, throwaway repos + a local clone of this repo): 20/20 (held, apply as it is, later edit applied, second function held, changed resend applied, module-level / nested def / non-Python / unparseable ranges, own lines applied, found 0 / 2 unchanged, fresh .git/agent, late run and no tag applied without history, real history = git log -L on the function). Sim (data/sim/armsim_r122.log, SIM_PRE_CMDS in armsim.py): a real first edit into fastapi/utils.py create_model_field is held with its real commits, the printed apply command applies it, 4/4 runs end ok, 54 requests carry each prompt exactly. Note for analysis: the held output contains 'NOT EDITED YET', so EDITED must be matched at the start of a line. **Bars (set now; 8 v 8):** sb1h ~1-3/8 on solves (luck). Gate: a held first edit in >= 40% of sbG runs with an edit (offline: ~55% of first edits come before 360 s). After a hold the next call is the resend or the apply command in >= 80% of holds; a hold never followed by any applied edit in that function <= 1 run. Use (the question): >= 1 resend changed in a way that follows a shown commit (hand-read), and the reasoning after a hold that shows a useful commit names it in >= 40%. Safety: runs changing an existing source file >= sb1h - 2; no-patch runs <= sb1h + 1; time ends <= sb1h + 2; agent s <= sb1h + 40; compactions <= sb1h + 1 per run (likely fails: prompt 2.1x sb1h). Decision: a resend that follows the history + safety -> the 12-task run; holds always applied as is / ignored -> history at the edit closes.
- **HISTORY-FIRST GATE: OFFLINE CHECK 2026-11-20 (user: 'working in both directions is good, try to start with it rather than late, and yes try building on sb1h because of it solving broken edits'; scripts/mine_bank/gate_check.py, log data/analysis/gate_check.log; CPU only, no git / network: timing from r118-r121 requests, functions + history from data/analysis/hist_window_cache.json).** Gate = sb1h's edit, but the FIRST edit into a function is not applied: the step prints that function's history (sbF2 filter) and asks for the edit again; later edits into a shown function apply as in sb1h. Bars set before: median extra calls <= 2 PASS (median 1, mean 1.39, max 5; 44/59 runs gate exactly one function); a useful commit at the gated first edit >= 30% PASS (25/59 = 42%: pyirf 4/4, pint-pandas 5/5, peewee 8/9, rich_3521 3/3, lizard-144 2/4, rich_3506 1/1, fastapi_11194 1/1, geopandas 1/2; 0 on fastapi_15589, policyuniverse, pySHACL, compress_pickle, rich_3676, lizard-370); late first edits <= 15% FAIL: the first edit comes late (median 330 s, p75 401, p90 453 of 480; call median 22, p90 29 of 28-call runs incl. the budget message) -> 31/84 runs (37%) edit first at >= 440 s or call >= 26, 9 of them solved; first edit at >= 400 s: 21 runs, 9 solved. A refused first edit costs one more reply (median 20 s, p75 30) + one call, so a plain gate would push many of these past the limit and risk up to ~9 of 45 solves. **Possible fix found in the harness source (not built, not yet verified on a run):** swegemma's setup ends with `git add -A && git commit -m eval_baseline --allow-empty && git tag -f _swegemma_baseline` (harness/container_setup.py setup_baseline_commit, the last step of setup_container_workspace), and the agent clock starts after setup (context.start_agent_session); so `$(date +%s) - $(git log -1 --format=%ct _swegemma_baseline)` should give the seconds since the task started -> a time-aware gate that holds back the first edit only while enough time is left (e.g. < 360 s; ~55% of first edits come before that). To verify first: the gap between the baseline commit and the agent's start, on the sim and on one Kaggle run.
- **RESULT r121 (data/results/2026-11-20_skill_r121_p1 = ziadsamer/fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu, _p2 = engziadsamer/arms-again; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r121.py X, log data/analysis/r121.log; no clones needed now: the labels file holds 'DATE TITLE' for every labelled commit, because Kaggle's task repos carry different hashes but the same dates and titles). This is exactly where the estimation happens.' (solved); rich_3521 p2 'Commit 353ebb66 simplified it slightly. Commit 643bf2b6 introduced it. My change removes this heuristic entirely.' (solved; sb1h 0/2 solved there). **CORRECTED 11-20 after reading the whole run: no second edit followed the history.** The model decided to replace the pos estimate with pos = 0 at 364 s, BEFORE the history arrived (391 s, after that edit); the later 'second edit' is the same edit re-applied after its own `git checkout` revert (to compare old and new output). So the >= 1 second edit bar FAILS (0 in r119-r121), and in all 5 runs the history only commented on an edit already made. **Why rich_3521 was solved once (the user asked 11-20):** the issue text is only 'Segment.split_cells fix' + a textual issue link, so both runs guessed the bug. p2 reproduced with 'AB' + 100 zero-width spaces, where the pos estimate (cut / cell_length * len(text)) lands far past the cut -> it removed the estimate -> passes the hidden test (mixed Japanese / ASCII text split at every position; the estimate overshoots there too). p1 reproduced with one combining accent ('a\u0301b'), a different bug (where a 0-width character goes), edited the two `cell_pos == cut` returns to skip 0-width characters, and the hidden test even asserts there are no 0-width characters; its history (showing the pos estimate commits = the real-fix line) arrived at 428 s of 480 and it went on with its plan. Which reproduction it happened to write decided the outcome; history played no part in either. fastapi x2: the edit already matched, straight to the check (the right branch, not named; both solved, as sb1h); rich_3521 p1 ignored it and went on with its plan (not solved). Naming after visible history 2/5 vs 0/7 for sbA + sbF without visible changes (p 0.15). **Cost:** compactions 2.8 vs 1.4 per run (+1.4, bar +1 FAIL; the prompt is 2,974 vs 1,512 words), but time ends 3 = 3, agent s 436 vs 439, runs changing an existing source file 5 vs 6 (all PASS); EDITED 6/8 heredoc edits (75%) vs 10/11. Pooled r120 + r121: sbF / sbF2 7/16 vs sb1h 4/16 (p 0.23, luck). Unlabelled commits shown (none useful by reading): rich_3521 '2024-09-28 docstring', rich_3506 'fix(types): remove a few more type ignores', 'Black formatting (#3035)'. **Per the bars (corrected): naming 2/5 passes by the letter, the second-edit bar FAILS: the history was read and named but changed no edit; history printed after the first edit can only help through a second edit, which never happened in r119-r121.** Note: the session scratchpad (clones, wt/ checkouts) was cleared on 11-20; offline git checks need a re-clone.

- **RESULT r120 (data/results/2026-11-19_skill_r120_p1 = ziadsamer/fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu, _p2 = engziadsamer/arms-again; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r120.py CLONE_DIR, log data/analysis/r120.log). Solved: sb1h 1/8, sbF 3/8 (luck range; sbF's 2 fastapi_11194 solves had the right edit BEFORE any history was printed).** **Delivery of titles passed, delivery of content failed (my design bug):** history after EDITED 4/4; a useful commit's TITLE shown in 4/8 runs (fastapi_11194 x2 'Fix form field regression', peewee p2 'Fixed bug foreign key cannot change rel model', rich_3521 p2 'fix split cells'; bar 4 PASS) — but `git log -L` on a whole function prints the whole function as context, so the 16-line cap per commit showed only the unchanged top of the function (signature, docstring) for every commit; the changed lines were never visible (fastapi: 3 commits with the same 13 opening lines). The offline window check counted commits by presence, not by visible change, so it could not catch this. **Use:** names a shown commit / case 0/4 by regex; read by me: both fastapi runs reasoned about it ('The history shows that first_field_info was introduced/maintained in several recent commits'; 'they all kept the first_field_info logic' = a wrong reading, the fix's change was hidden), peewee / rich went straight to the check; second edit following history 0 (bar >= 1 FAIL). **Cost:** compactions 2.2 vs 1.1 per run (+1.1, bar +1: FAIL by a hair), time ends 4 = 4 PASS, agent s 414 vs 406 PASS, runs changing an existing source file 4 vs 3 PASS; EDITED 4/6 vs 8/9. **Hash note:** Kaggle's task repos carry different commit hashes from the public clones (rewritten history; e.g. 'Fix form field regression' 4d1c0d0 on Kaggle = 88d4f2c in the clone), so run analyses must match commits by title (r120.py does; r119.py's 'real-fix commit shown' matched by hash and may undercount). **Fix found offline (not built):** keep only each commit's +/- lines (`grep -E '^(commit |[-+])'` after the existing filter): fastapi_11194 then shows 'Fix form field regression: - values[field.name] = value / + values[field.alias] = value', rich_3521 the `pos = int((cut / cell_length) ...)` changes (the buggy line), peewee abefa44's rel_model lines; outputs get shorter. The history-at-the-edit question (does the model act on a useful change it can SEE?) is still untested.

Examples: 3 = edit -> same-kind commit (ParseError) -> second edit copied from the new text -> no-history line -> check; 4 = found 0 -> re-read -> edit -> unrelated history (closing the file, a move) -> check; 5 = found 2 -> def line added -> history whose newest commit dropped item.quantity (the regression) -> the edit already undoes it -> check; + new file / cut-apart / budget. Use (the question): in runs that got a useful commit, the next reply names a shown commit or its case in >= 40%; >= 1 run makes a second edit that follows a shown commit (hand-read). - **RESULT r119 (data/results/2026-11-19_skill_r119_p1 = ziadsamer/fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu, _p2 = engziadsamer/arms-again; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r119.py CLONE_DIR, log data/analysis/r119.log). Solved: sb1h 3/8, sbA 1/8, sbB 2/8 (all luck: bank 34% gives P(<= 1) 0.18 for sbA). Both history arms FAIL their mechanism bars; neither goes to the 12-task run.** **sbA (history printed by the edit):** the command works on Kaggle: every EDITED edit printed history with commits (6/6, ~2,200 chars, no git errors, no extra seconds: the reply after an edit took 24-35 s as usual). But the model never used it: in the 3 runs that got history the next reply named no shown commit and no case (0/3, bar 40%); no second edit followed history (0, bar >= 1); the replies just go to the next planned edit ('Now update model_to_code'). Read by me: the 6 history outputs were unrelated to the fix (Pydantic v2 / Annotated support, 'Fix #96' repr default, 'Fix auto migrations' kwargs) except rich p1 ('fix split cells', solved anyway), so moving on was the right branch, but the model never said so. Cost: prompt 3,225 words vs sb1h 1,512 -> compactions 3.1/run vs 1.5 (each a ~25-35 s summary call that also drops context) -> agent s 465 vs 397 (+68, bar +40 FAIL), time ends 6/8 vs 2/8 (bar +2 FAIL, Fisher p 0.07), runs that changed an existing source file 3/8 vs 6/8 (p 0.16); non-EDITED edit results 45% vs 33% (bar +10 FAIL, 5/11 vs 5/15). **sbB (history printed by the read, read_file removed):** the model read code with only the first line of the read command, plain `sed -n N,Mp FILE`, in 71 of 73 reads (read command 2/73 = 3%, bar 80% FAIL); a real-fix commit before the first edit 0/8 (bar 4 FAIL); heredoc edits 0.6/run; existing source changed in 3/8 runs; agent s 469 (+72 FAIL), time ends 4/8; compactions 1.9/run (prompt 2,654 words). **Conclusions:** (1) the model drops any extra part of a command it can shorten (sbB) = r118's lesson again: a multi-line command copied from the prompt is not followed; only output the harness forces on it arrives (sbA's history arrived 6/6). (2) Arrived history is still not acted on (sbA 0/3; sb7h r118 3/24; r116-r117 ~0-4/24), BUT r119 never delivered relevant history, so it did not test use: all 4 tasks have related commits on the real-fix lines (fastapi 'Fix parameter aliases' / 'Fix Query/Header/Cookie parameter model alias'; geopandas 'PERF: improve frame_join' = the regression; peewee 'Fixed bug foreign key cannot change rel model'; rich 'fix split cells' 2024 / 'fix issue splitting segments' 2023). Why none arrived (user asked 11-19): geopandas sbA never edited (time); fastapi's edit near the real-fix lines got 'found 0' (no history after a failed edit), the EDITED one was in another function (Pydantic v2 history); rich's edit was inside _split_cells but its 2-3 replaced lines were last changed in 2021, the related 2023/2024 fixes touched the neighbouring lines (window too narrow); peewee p2 DID edit the real-fix line (auto.py:70 self.rel_model in Column.__init__), but its old text spanned more lines, so the 2 newest commits on that range were 'Fix #96' and 'Peewee 3.3.3 tests are passed' and the related 'Fixed bug foreign key cannot change rel model' (2nd on line 70 alone) was cut by -n 2 (corrected 11-19; fastapi's failed edit verified at utils.py:821-823, the real fix at 826-829 in the same function). A wider window adds more commits competing for the same 2 slots, so 'whole function' is not automatically better. Design weak points: history keyed on the exact replaced lines needs an exact, successful edit of the right lines; candidate fix = the history of the whole enclosing function (offline check on the bank's edits first, proposed, not run). (3) Prompt length has a measured time cost through compaction (token_threshold 14,336 fixed by the harness): doubling the prompt doubled compactions; any new arm should keep the sb1h prompt size. (4) Parser lessons (in r119.py): tool results are stored with escaped newlines; compaction summary requests (one message) sit between a call and its result.

(instruction in the header line) + git log -L of the replaced lines in HEAD (2 commits x 16 lines), or '== NO HISTORY FOR THESE LINES (new or already edited lines): go on to the check.' after an edit of lines not in HEAD; nothing after a failed edit; exit 0 always; examples: same-kind fix -> second edit (ParseError), regression -> re-read + second edit (sorted()), unrelated -> check, no history -> check, + sb1h's found 0 / found 2 / new file / cut-apart / budget. **sbB** = sb1h with read_file removed; every read = `sed -n S,Ep FILE` + header + git log -L S,E:FILE (same cap); edits = sb1h's step byte for byte; examples: same-kind fix -> edit that follows it, regression -> edit that corrects it, unrelated, 'There is no path ... in the commit' (new file), 'fatal: file X has only N lines' (start after the end; an end after the end is accepted by git) -> read inside, + found 0 (read again) / found 2 / new file / cut-apart / budget. Clean: no other change (user). Checks (scripts/mine_bank/hist_ab_cmd_check.py, log data/analysis/hist_ab_cmd_check.log): A on the real fixes of 15 tasks = real patch 15/15, 31/31 hunks exit 0 with history; second edit of just-added lines -> the no-history line 14/14; old text not found -> no history line, exit 0; a new untracked file -> no-history line; B on r118's 228 ranged reads: exit 0 with the header 228, sed lines = the file's lines 228; both git messages match the examples. An arm whose mechanism bars pass goes to the 12-task run (both accounts).
- **HISTORY AT THE EDIT / AT THE READ: OFFLINE CHECK + PLAN A and B, 2026-11-19 (user: 'yes run the offline check first, then plan both A and B'; scripts/mine_bank/hist_at_edit_check.py, log data/analysis/hist_at_edit_check.log; CPU, git / bash / our own fixed Python step on the sparse checkouts; not built).** Why: every earlier try picked history from the issue's names before the location was known (real-fix function 8-13/40) or put it in the prompt / a pinned stage (cited 1-4/24); the model reacts to tool results. **A** = sb1h's heredoc edit whose fixed step also writes the old text's line range in HEAD to .git/agent/range and, after the diff, prints '== HOW THESE LINES CHANGED BEFORE' = git log -L on those lines, 2 commits, 16 lines each (no double quotes / backticks / backslashes; braces only awk's {n=0}). **B** = read_file removed; reading = `sed -n S,Ep FILE` (same text as read_file, which prints no line numbers: swegemma tools/workspace.py) + the same history of lines S-E. **Results:** (1) A on the real fixes of 15 tasks: 31/31 hunks EDITED (= real patch), history printed 31/31, a commit of the real-fix lines 25/31; output 1,603 chars mean (history 831); 0.08 s median, 3.1 s max. My reading (judgement) of the first hunk's history: related 9/15 (rich_3521 'fix split cells' on the same pos formula, geopandas the PERF regression commit, fastapi_15589 'Fix parameter aliases', rich_3278 'fix for ansi issue' on the same regex, peewee 'Fixed bug foreign key ...', rich_3676 the error_range style commits, rich_3506 'fix issue splitting segments', pint-pandas the ndim check in _convert_np_result, pySHACL the _found_child_bnodes commits), maybe 2 (pyirf empty-input NaNs, fastapi_11194 the embed refactor), unrelated 4 (lizard x2 'restructure' new file, policyuniverse data loading, compress_pickle imports). (2) A on the model's own r118 edits (sb1h + sb1hpp, 70 heredoc edits): old text found once in HEAD 50, history printed 50/50, a real-fix commit 29; history 728 chars median (max 1,818); 0.06 s median, 4.3 s max. Per run (36 with an edit): the FIRST edit shows a real-fix commit 24/36, any edit 25/36. (3) B on the model's own r118 reads (363 read_file calls; 135 = 37% whole-file reads without a range): ranged 228, history printed 228, real-fix commit 118; output 2,404 chars median (history part 1,162 median, 2,246 max: about +1.2k chars per read -> earlier compaction is B's main cost); 0.03 s median, 17.1 s max; 0 git errors. Per run (46): a ranged read before the first edit 40, one of them shows a real-fix commit 32/46 (vs sb7h's stage: real-fix function pinned 3/24). **PLAN (both A and B; arms on sb1h, which is the control):** **sbA** = sb1h with the A edit; prompt written fresh; the history header line in the tool output carries the instruction (event): '== HOW THESE LINES CHANGED BEFORE, newest first. If one of these commits fixed the same kind of problem, make your edit the same way; if the newest one made the change the issue complains about, your edit corrects it'. Worked examples, one per case, each showing the full edit output with history and the next call: (a) a past fix of the same kind -> a second edit that follows it (same helper / check / place); (b) the newest commit is the regression -> the edit is checked against it and corrected; (c) formatting / move / unrelated -> straight to the check; (d) 'no history' (new file or old text not in HEAD) -> straight to the check; plus sb1h's found 0 / found 2 / new file / cut-apart / budget examples. Weakness: history arrives after the first edit, so it helps only through a second edit. **sbB** = sb1h with read_file removed (structure) and the B read command; edits = sb1h's (no history at the edit, so A and B differ in one thing); prompt written fresh; the same event header and cases (a)-(d) shown on read outputs, the case decided BEFORE the edit; plus 'git: file X has only N lines' -> re-run with E = N (example). Weakness: +1.2k chars per read (compaction sooner), and whole-file reads are gone (finding stays git grep). Optional sbAB (both) only if the user wants a 4th arm. Both: harness tools only, run_command, no skill script (scorer risk above), no budgets. **Checks before the run:** builder asserts (fixed steps byte for byte, old text copied from the read output before it, no double quotes / backticks / backslashes in shell blocks, braces only {n=0}); the A command on the 15 real fixes = real patch again; sim (venv_harness). **Run:** sb1h vs sbA vs sbB on r117 / r118's 12 tasks, both accounts = 24 runs per arm, guard 2.8 h. **Bars (set before the run):** solves inside +-5 of sb1h = luck (expected sb1h 11-13/24). sbA: history printed after >= 60% of EDITED edits; a reply after a history output names a shown commit or its case in >= 40% of runs that got history; a second edit of the same lines right after a history output in >= 15% of runs; time ends <= sb1h + 2; agent s <= sb1h + 25. sbB: >= 90% of reads through the B command; a real-fix commit shown before the first edit in >= 50% of runs (offline 70%); 'old text found 0 times' share <= sb1h's + 10 points; compactions per run <= sb1h + 1; time ends <= sb1h + 3; agent s <= sb1h + 30.
- **AFTER r118 2026-11-19 (user: 'this pp drop it, i still believe we need to play abit to help the history ... maybe the way we use it is the problem; i like how broken calls reduced that much, i need the time of that as well; am okay with the skill script try, i am afraid it times out on the scorer, check what people say, maybe we build the agent from scratch to be history dependent or think together').** **Presence penalty CLOSED (user).** **Broken-call time** (scripts/mine_bank/broken_call_time.py, log data/analysis/broken_call_time.log; cost = seconds of the reply that produced a call whose result says 'mandatory input parameters'): bank sb1 238 runs: 2.32 broken calls / 27.4 s per run = 7.6% of model time; in the 65 runs with any, 100 s mean, max 413 s; r118 sb1 40.1 s per run (11.7%), 126 s in its 7 runs; sb1h / sb1hpp / sb7h 0. sb1h's agent s is only 16 s lower than sb1 in r118 (342 vs 358) because it makes more calls (23.9 vs 19.6 per run). **Skill script on the scorer:** our LB: one skill call per run fitted 12 h every time (evidc 0.15 / 0.12, sk_a2 0.18, sk_a2_32 0.17); heavy use (13-14 per run) 4/4 over; evid3 (one call, 40 s script deadline) over. Forum (read 2026-11-19, no staff answer yet): 758547 a skill-only bundle scores 0.00 on the LB but works in local Docker mode; 758854 a team found their BM25 skill script indexed its own skill folder, not the task repo ('skill scripts seem to run outside the agent's sandbox', their reading). Harness source we hold (swegemma 0.2.7 agent_runner.py 327: AdkSandboxCodeExecutor with the task sandbox, script written into work_dir and run there) says scripts run inside the sandbox; the scorer's version is newer (unverified). So a skill script is likely fine on time with one short call, but whether it sees the repo on the scorer is UNVERIFIED -> prefer run_command designs for history.
- **RESULT r118 (data/results/2026-11-19_skill_r118_p1 = ziadsamer/fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu, _p2 = engziadsamer/arms-again; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r118.py, log data/analysis/r118.log). Solved: sb1 12/22, sb1h 11/22, sb1hpp 10/24, sb7h 7/24** (the 2.8 h guard cut lizard-144 for sb1 / sb1h in both passes). **sb1h: the cut-apart loops are gone; solves = sb1 (luck).** Cut-apart results per run 4.73 -> 0.00 (runs with any 7/22 -> 0/22, Fisher p ~0.009; sb1's longest loop 35 calls); time ends 7 -> 3 (sb1's 7 sits at the top of the bank sb1 range [1, 6], sb1h's 3 is ordinary, p 0.48); no patch 5 = 5; runs that change an existing source file 14/22 vs 13/22; agent s 342 vs 358. Bars: cut-apart <= 0.5 PASS, time ends PASS, no patch PASS, agent s PASS, heredoc edits printing EDITED >= 70% FAIL (61%: 19 EDITED, 7 'found 0 times' (2 fixed by the next edit, 2 runs never), 4 bash 'unexpected EOF while looking for matching ``' = the edit sent inside its ``` fence lines, 1 budget exhausted). **sb1hpp vs sb1h: the penalty does nothing useful now.** Tokens per reply 355 vs 363 (0.98x, bar 0.85 FAIL), long-reply share 16% vs 21% (-5 points, bar -8 FAIL), cut-apart 0 (PASS: the heredoc removed the long strings the penalty broke), time ends 4 vs 3 (FAIL by 1), EDITED 61%, found-0 never fixed in 4 runs. **sb7h: worse than every arm and below sb1's bank range on solves (7 vs [10, 17], p < 0.01); the stage broke again.** 16/24 runs never changed a source file (patches of only scratch files like reproduce_issue.py); time ends 12/24. Stage: <= 2 requests 8/24 (bar 20 FAIL), mean 184 s (bar 40 FAIL), 189 of 256 stage calls sent inside the ``` fence lines in 15/24 runs, and after the bash error the model resends the same fenced command up to 23 times (example 3's recovery was not followed; its reasoning blames echo / the quotes instead of the fence); the stage ended with real history 14/24 (bar 18 FAIL), 'No history' 9 (5 of them after error loops), 1 ran out. Fixer names a pinned commit before its first edit 3/24 (bar 9 FAIL; r117 4/24, r116 ~0). Real-fix function among the pinned 3/24. **Conclusions:** (1) heredoc edits are the better edit tool: sb1h = new base for the next arms (no solve gain shown; its gain is the removed failure loop and fewer time ends). Its two left-over edit failures each need a worked example + a WHEN A TOOL RESULT SAYS line: the fence-copy bash error ('unexpected EOF while looking for matching'), and 'found 0 times' -> read_file again. (2) presence penalty closed: no length effect left once edits are heredocs. (3) A long command copied from a prompt fails in every layout tried (fenced: copied fences, sb7f 4/8, sb7h 15/24; indented: typed calls / split commands, sb7g) -> the history command must not be typed by the model: evidc's precedent is one run_skill_script call per run (skills/.../scripts/*.py), which the model sent cleanly; a skill script that takes the names as arguments removes the copy step. (4) Even with history pinned, the fixer cites it in 3-4 of 24 runs across r116-r118: adoption is the second open problem of the history line.

- **BROKEN TOOL CALLS ANALYSIS 2026-11-19 (user: 'explain the cut-apart ... before trying 1.0 see where we can change'; scripts/mine_bank/broken_calls.py, log data/analysis/broken_calls.log).** Gemma 4 wraps every string argument in one special token <|"|> (ai.google.dev Gemma 4 prompt formatting; vLLM gemma4 tool parser); a call breaks when the model closes a long string (mostly new_string, written before old_string, so old_string goes missing) with a backtick or a double quote instead of <|"|>: the parser then splits the code at its ':' / ',' and a required argument is missing. Bank: sb1 breaks 43% of its edit_file / write_file calls (393 / 910), pub024 31%, sa2 32%; the stray closer is a backtick in every arm (model habit, not only our fences), but sb1's FIRST edit call breaks 23% vs sa2 9% / pub024 12% (fenced old_string examples may add to it); after a broken call the next one breaks again 89-93% in every arm (the model copies its own broken call from the context: the loops). presence_penalty 1.5: broken 79%, first edit call broken 54%, filepath intact in 124/137 (the first short string survives, the later long one breaks) - fits vLLM's penalty hitting <|"|> (apply_penalties masks every output token, special ones not excluded; source read 11-19, the Kaggle vLLM version not checked). History arms: the fixers ran 0 git history commands of their own (sb7s / sb7f / sb7g); the repeat calls were in the history STAGE after its DONE / FETCHED output (sb7f 3 runs, sb7g 4).

Lessons: commands in stage examples - fenced -> fence copied (sb7f), indented -> delimiters dropped / call typed as text (sb7g); long copied commands are fragile either way.

Same starvation as sb3-sb5 (r108-r110). Outcome difference is no evidence about history itself: the history rarely reached a stage that still had time. Lessons: (1) structure guarantees that a stage RUNS, not that it stops; a one-call stage needs its own stop signal in its tool output; (2) the 8-minute cap leaves no room for a second stage that needs real calls (third time: sb3-5, sb8).

- **RESULT testcheck (CPU notebook ziad23samer/regrade-cpu; data/results/2026-11-17_testcheck_rt; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/testcheck_result.py): pre-set bar MISSED narrowly; the idea is risky because real fixes often break old tests.** 215 rows, hook fired 215, apply failures 0, base by reverse apply 37 (cached 131). NEW failure: wrong 31/140 = 22.1% (bar >= 20% met; 25/110 of those touching a real-fix file), solved 3/51 = 5.9% (bar <= 5% missed by one patch), real fixes 6/24 = 25%. Split by the real fix: in the 14 tasks where it keeps the picked tests passing, wrong patches break a test 18/89 (on 5 tasks: pyirf 5/5, compress_pickle 6/6, lizard-174 3/7, geopandas 2/3, whatshap 2/4) and solved 0/26 = a clean signal; in the 6 tasks where the real fix itself breaks old tests (openhands-resolver, hpc-container-maker, qiskit-bot, diy-googledrive, lightstep, sievelib: tests that encode the old behaviour, rewritten by the hidden test patch), solved 3/6 and wrong 13/37 break them, so 'make the old tests pass' would push the agent away from the right fix there. Rough net per 48 test-set runs: ~4-5 wrong runs caught (if the agent then fixes 30-50%: +1.5-2.5) vs ~4 solved runs at risk in the old-behaviour tasks (if 25-50% get undone: -1 to -2) -> about zero unless the agent can tell an old-behaviour test from a real regression. Picks: hidden-test file among the picks for 20/24 real fixes; nothing picked on pySHACL, meson, lizard-144, lizard-370; 28 wrong patches had no source edit.
- **RUN BUILT testcheck (CPU, no model): does running the EXISTING tests of the edited code tell wrong patches from right ones? kaggle/cells_testcheck_rt (scripts/train/make_testcheck_cells.py), user 2026-11-17: 'yes build the CPU check'.** For the 24 SWE-rebench test tasks: 191 bank patches (scripts/mine_bank/testcheck_patches.py: per task <= 10 wrong + <= 5 solved, deduplicated, current line first, > 30k-char data patches dropped; 140 wrong, 111 of them touch a real-fix file; 51 solved) + the 24 real fixes. Each goes through the scorer's own verify_task (same workspace build); its hidden-test pytest call is hooked: the test-picking command a future agent would run (scripts/agent_tc/testsel_cmd.sh: edited source files -> test files named after them, test files importing the module, test files using the edited def / class names; top 3; then timeout 150 pytest -q -rfE) runs on the patched tree, then the same files with the patch reverse-applied (the base; stash fallback; cached per task + file set). NEW failure = fails with the patch, not at the base. Hidden tests are never applied (test_patch emptied). Local git-only check of the picking on the clones: the real fix's picks include the hidden-test file in 20/24 tasks (none picked: pySHACL, meson, lizard-144, lizard-370), wrong patches 104/140, solved 32/51; no-source-edit patches 25 (pySHACL scratch-only mostly). Dry run with a fake harness (scripts/train/test_testcheck_cells.py, canned pytest output, real git on the clones): hook fires, base by reverse apply, rows, per-patch lines and summary OK. **Bars (set now):** wrong patches with a NEW failure >= 20% AND solved patches <= 5% -> the idea has room (next: a single-agent arm whose post-edit command prints the failing tests with a NEXT line); otherwise drop it. Also read: real fixes with a NEW failure (old tests that encode the old behaviour would mislead the agent), base already failing (environment noise). Runs on a CPU session of a notebook pinned to the original environment (Python 3.12), internet off, deadline 9 h from cell 2, 3 at a time; estimate 2-4 h. Inputs: competition, wheelhouse, rodrigospalma/ood45-bugfix-split, and the task set https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/ziadsamer/rebench-new-cp312 (holds rebench/tasks.jsonl, verify_gate.json, snapshots, cp312 wheels; cell 2 needs exactly one verify_gate.json among the inputs); no model.
- **DIRECTION 3 RESEARCH: WRONG PATCHES 2026-11-17 (user: 'yes start the search for direction 3'; scripts/mine_bank/wrong_vs_solved.py, wrong_patch_shape.py, wrong_cases.py, nopatch_edit_errors.py, calls_by_outcome.py).** (1) Of the current line's 181 wrong patches, 27 have no real source change (only repro.py / check.py / data files): they are no-edit runs (slice 1). On the test set 107 of 132 wrong patches touch a real-fix file: the change is wrong or incomplete. (2) The agent's own check passes on most wrong patches: only 129 of 1,712 patched runs ended with an error in their last check -> it tests the agent's reading of the issue, not the hidden test's. (3) Case review (10 test tasks with most wrong patches): (a) hidden test checks behaviour the issue never states (sievelib error texts, diy-googledrive lazy auth, msal token_source): near unwinnable; (b) new machinery instead of a small change to what is there (wemake allow-list, peewee field.model vs a new model= parameter, qiskit-bot ignores the issue's own 'second API request' suggestion); (c) a new feature placed or shaped unlike its siblings (data-morph shapes/ vs shapes/points/, fromager output header). Patch size does NOT separate wrong from solved within a task (22 tasks: changed lines 5.5 vs 6.5; >2x the real fix 0.67 vs 0.67); changed def lines 19 of 181 wrong patches, a rare cause. (4) Same-task behaviour (all 141 agents, 1,712 patched runs): every check behaviour goes with LOWER solving (struggling runs check more: reverse causation). Current line only (413 runs): pytest after the last edit +0.18 (9 tasks better / 2 worse), validation share >= 25% +0.16 (7/1), repro before the edit +0.15 (5/2); sb1 r104: pytest after the last edit solved 14/26 vs 6/22, the only sb1 mechanism that moved. (5) Outside evidence: TestPrune (arXiv 2510.18270): the agent runs a small selected set of EXISTING tests at the start and after each candidate patch -> +8-9% relative resolution for SWE-agent and Trae Agent (Claude 3.7 / Sonnet 4; no open models tested). Behavioral drivers study (arXiv 2604.02547, 9,374 trajectories): validation share correlates with success (rho +0.50; strong agents 35-37% of steps, weak 12-19%), the prompt matters more for weaker models (+20 pp vs +4 pp), but these are agent-level correlations. Test-overfitting study (arXiv 2511.16858): even the gold tests add only ~3% for Claude. ETH 'agents fix correct code': reproduce-first plus an explicit abstain option changed behaviour; reproduce-first alone was mixed.

- **RESULT r109 (data/results/2026-11-17_skill_sb4h12_p1 = fatmazenhom/generation-shard-4-gpu, _p2 = ziad23samer/fork-of-fork-of-generation-all-shards-gpu; python3 -I scripts/mine_bank/r109_sb4.py): sb4 9/24 vs sb1 10/24 (luck; tasks +3 / -4). The post-edit history check is closed as an effect on these tasks.** (1) Hand-over: fixer calls median 23 (bar <= 18; sb3 ~28). The fixer DOES stop soon after its first edit (1-5 calls later in 14 of 19 runs with an edit; 11-24 later in 5), but the first edit itself comes late (call ~14-20, 100-270 s); 5 runs never edited (geopandas x2, rich_3521 x2, rich_4079 p1; all time ends). Cause found in sa1's own runs (bad_by_agent.py --only=2026-11-16_skill_sa1rt): garbled edit calls in 22 of 24 sa1 runs (135 calls, ~75 s/run) vs 3 of 24 pub024 runs: Example 2 wrote the edit as a Python-style call with escaped strings (\n, \"), the model copied that escaping into its native calls, and the call parser split the arguments at quotes / colons inside the code (only 31 of ~150 sa1 edit calls well formed; keys like 'params = {\\n \\"api-version\\"').** Worked: Example 1 (heredoc python): inline python broken 0 of 24 sa1 runs vs 4 of 24 pub024; /tmp writes 3 vs 2; edit from memory 1 vs 3. Lesson (now in the bank): a few-shot example of a tool call must not show escaped string literals or Python call syntax; show the arguments as plain text blocks with real newlines. sa1's 8 vs 10 is confounded by this self-inflicted bug; the rest of its prompt is untested.

The empirical rule stands: heavy skill use 4 of 4 over 12 h; one setup call (ska2) fine.

Tested and ruled out now: 'heavy-skill agents simply use more time per task': local mean wall per task sq1c2 355 s and sq1c2t7 321 s (both over 12 h on the LB) vs pub024 382 s and combo1 388 s (both scored); sk_a2 324, evidc 394, evid3 413 (over), srep7 408 (over), nn32 407 (over). So sq1c2 / sq1c2t7 cost more on the scorer than locally: to exceed 12 h they needed >= ~360 s per task there (~+40-60 s per task = ~3-5 s per skill call at 13-14 calls), an inference. Also: pub024's 120 x local wall = 12.7 h, so all our 8-min agents sit near the limit and small scorer-side costs tip them over. Light, inside the single agent (1.0 skill call per run = 4%): sk_a2 11, sk_a2_32 10; ska2 also ran its a.py through run_command in 68% of its calls (16.6 per run) and never ran over. None: pub024 11, combo1 9 (mix 10, r4_budget 9: no local logs). Reading: scripts via the native run_command cost nothing visible on the LB; the cost sits in run_skill_script calls (the sandbox executor's write + python3 + delete per call), consistent with the Docker-overhead hypothesis. A minimal one-call setup skill inside a single agent is consistent with the best scores; heavy skill use is not. Possible rule-1 variant offered to the user: harness tools plus at most one setup skill call per run, everything else through the harness tools.
- **WHY STRUCTURED AGENTS LOSE ON THE LB 2026-11-15 (user: 'why ... skills ... on the leaderboard, what may be the cause'; analysis only).** Correction to PROMPT REVIEW (1): ska2 / ska232 (11 and 10 of 58) DO use a skill (install a.py once, ~1.1 skill calls per run), so the split is not skills vs none. By structure: one LlmAgent with the harness tools (mix, ska2, ska232, pub024, combo1, r4_budget): 0 of 6 over the 12 h limit, mean 10.0/58 when scored; staged SequentialAgents, a sub-agent, or every command through a skill shell (sq1c2, sq1c2t7, srep7, evid3, le5, nn32, evidc x2, evidefs, nn32rcn2, sq1c2a6 x2, le6): 6 of 13 over 12 h, mean 7.3/58 when scored. Locally the same agents fit 8 min and some had our best local rates (sq1c2 72% on its tasks), so they cost more time on the scorer than in our notebooks. Ruled out: harness nudges re-running the evidence stage (6 of 205 evidc runs, 3%, ~3 s). Measured for all agents: compaction summary calls ~29-35 s of model time per evidc run. Remaining causes, untested: (a) skill calls in the scorer's Docker sandbox = write a .py into /workspace + a new python3 process + a delete, three container operations per call (adk_eval_core sandbox/base.py AdkSandboxCodeExecutor), multiplied by ~25-30 calls in skill-shell agents; our notebooks use sandbox='subprocess', so we never measured it; (b) stage scripts that scale with repo size (evid3's 40 s evidence deadline on large private repos); (c) stage model calls inside the 8-min cap. For agents that fit but scored lower (evidc 9 / 7 vs pub024 11): stage time inside the cap, or dev16 selection not transferring; r99 (pub024 vs evidc on 21 other repos) separates the two. Rule 1 (harness tools only) stays the user's rule; the evidence behind it is 'one agent, no stages, no skill shell'.
